# Paperplane v23 Launch Audit — Current Status (2026-11-22)

## Release verdict: HOLD — not deployment-ready

**Current checkpoint (2026-11-22):** v23 register is 325 FIXED LOCALLY, 27 PARTIAL, and 1 BLOCKED (353 total). ID 27 is now verified against the deployed v154/CSS v61 site across all 44 routes at seven widths for two rounds (616 checks, zero failures or page errors; `browser-id27-live-layout-v154-2026-11-22.json`). Fresh full pytest passed 647 tests twice on the current source, with four existing dependency deprecation warnings per run (run 1: 356.59s; run 2: 309.97s; `fresh-verification-2026-11-22.json`). The P0 Chromium matrix passed three current-source rounds at 60/60 each with zero page errors (`browser-p0-round116.json`, `browser-p0-round117.json`, `browser-p0-round118.json`); the current-source 44-route responsive matrix passed 704/704 at eight widths and two rounds (`browser-id27-narrow-zoom-layout.json`); app.js v152/shell v93 cold-offline merge passed twice with content-checked PDFs and zero job posts/errors (`browser-id357-offline-local-merge.json`). Exact production image `paperplane-v23-audit:codex-v161` (digest `sha256:231cc30ca94616db5024d40084827e2a1410e57e04d890036ab3d1be98611fd2`) passed health with four UID-1001 workers, production-image offline merge/PWA, two content-checked 69-page Studio exports, and the full 44-operation catalog twice (41 outputs content-checked per round, two provider-only operations unavailable, SSRF blocked, zero other failures/page errors). The Docker 69-page max tasks were 420ms/328ms, exceeding the 250ms ID 122 target; ID 122 remains PARTIAL. The batch-4 page-slot experiment was rejected after it materially regressed readiness/export and long tasks; the shipped candidate retains batch-16. A module-script parse trial was also rejected: the Studio harness passed with temporary exports, but the P0 harness exposed hidden global dependencies (`showSummaryDeskResult`); the current app remains a classic script. Provider/live, owner/legal, and physical-device checks remain. No commit, push, deploy, or real payment occurred. Evidence: `docker-v161-runtime.json`, `browser-v161-docker-offline-pwa.json`, `browser-id122-mobile-69page-studio-export_docker_v161.json`, `browser-id28-v161-current-image-catalog-current-v161-2026-11-22.json`.

**Studio worker integration and production-image check (2026-11-21, app.js v145 / page_organizer.js v20 / pdf_runtime.js v25 / shell v85):** Reproduction showed Studio was silently falling back because PDF.js 3.x tried to call `document.createElement("canvas")` inside the OffscreenCanvas worker. Added a worker-only canvas surface backed by OffscreenCanvas, prevented a failed thumbnail request from closing the shared Studio session, captured worker failure diagnostics, and made the browser harness require that the worker really stays active. Two 390px Chromium runs on the real 69-page `notes.pdf` preserved all page text/order, rendered pages 1 and 40, passed selection/navigation/slot recovery, and had zero page or console errors. The maximum main-thread long tasks were 428ms and 616ms, above the 250ms target. A disposable production-mode four-worker Docker image v151 reached healthy status; two equivalent browser rounds preserved output content/order with no errors and an active worker, but maximum tasks were 879ms and 533ms. This fixes the worker fallback and shared-session teardown bugs but does not close ID 122; physical low-end device and <250ms performance checks remain open. Full current-source pytest passed **637 tests twice** with four existing warnings per run (`pytest-current-v161-final-run1.log`, `pytest-current-v161-final-run2.log`). The previous v161 attempt had two stale v23 runtime-version assertions; those tests were updated and the clean reruns passed. Register remains **324 FIXED LOCALLY, 28 PARTIAL, 1 BLOCKED** (353 items); release verdict remains HOLD. Evidence: `browser-id122-mobile-69page-studio-export_worker_canvas_shim_final_20261121.json`, `browser-id122-mobile-69page-studio-export_v151_docker_20261121.json`, `docker-id122-v151-runtime.json`, `docker-build-v151.log`.

**Post-v145 regressions:** Current-source P0 browser round 105 completed **60/60 checks** with zero page errors (`browser-p0-round105.json`). The 44-route responsive matrix completed **704/704** checks over eight widths and two rounds (`browser-id27-narrow-zoom-layout.json`). Production Docker image v151 shell v85 passed two offline Chromium rounds, serving app v145, CSS v61, organizer v20, PDF runtime v25, and the Studio render worker v4 at 390px with HTTP 200 and zero page errors (`browser-v151-docker-offline-pwa.json`). The disposable v151 container was removed after verification.

**Cold-offline PDF processing fix (2026-11-21, service-worker shell v86):** Self-test found that the offline shell loaded but local Merge failed because PDF.js, its parser worker, and PDF-lib were not precached; the app then prompted for a server upload even though Local mode was selected. Added those three dependencies to the PWA shell (the local PDF worker was already cached) and added a regression assertion. Two current-source Chromium rounds and two production Docker v152 rounds disabled networking, opened `/merge-pdf`, merged two synthetic one-page PDFs, and downloaded a content-checked two-page result with ordered markers. Each round returned HTTP 200, made zero `/api/jobs` submissions, and had zero page errors. In both Docker rounds, the same offline page then opened a two-page PDF in Studio, rendered page 1, and kept the OffscreenCanvas worker active. The v152 image was healthy with four UID-1001 workers; its temporary container and tmpfs were removed. Evidence: `browser-id357-offline-local-merge.json`, `browser-v152-docker-offline-pwa.json`, `docker-v152-offline-runtime.json`, `docker-build-v152.log`. ID 357 remains PARTIAL for native camera/install behavior on physical Android/iOS; overall release remains HOLD.

**Post-v86 full-suite verification:** the current source passed **638 tests twice**, with four existing deprecation warnings per run (`pytest-current-v162-run1.log`: 246.91s; `pytest-current-v162-run2.log`: 482.96s). This supersedes v161 for the v86 shell-cache change; it does not clear the Studio performance or physical-device checks.

**Read-only live drift/availability check (2026-11-21):** A Chromium load of `https://paperplane.qd.je` returned HTTP 200 and exposed deployed assets app.js v91, page organizer v14, PDF runtime v18, and CSS v51; current local assets are v145/v20/v25/v61. On a later attempt the root response and CSS/theme assets returned 200, but DOMContentLoaded timed out at 30s, a direct app.js v91 request timed out at 10s, and `/api/health` timed out at 8s. No page errors were recorded. This is read-only evidence and no live account, job, or billing mutation occurred. The current local candidate is not deployed, and live site availability needs owner-side follow-up. Evidence: `browser-live-assets-readonly-2026-11-21.json`.

**Translation repair cap follow-up (2026-11-21, backend):** Self-analysis found the repair loop capped recovery at four small provider calls, which left two of ten segments untranslated after a fully failed batch. The bounded cap is now 16 calls and repeats while it recovers segments, still stopping on zero progress or the document deadline. Regression tests assert both complete recovery of ten segments and multi-round 3+2+1 recovery when each repair returns only one segment. Two local Chromium rounds on `/translate-pdf` forced an initial HTTP 504, then verified five smaller repair requests, HTTP 200, 100% text/character coverage, all ten Spanish page markers in order, source text removed, content-checked downloads, and no browser errors. Latest full suite passed **636 tests twice** with four existing warnings per run (`pytest-current-v158-summary.json`). A dedicated progress-round regression verifies one-at-a-time partial repair continues through 3+2+1 requests. ID 1 remains PARTIAL because the independent live Hindi/Spanish probe still returned 429/422; this controlled-provider test proves the local repair path, not live provider stability. Evidence: `browser-id1-failed-batch-repair.json`, `pytest-current-v158-summary.json`.

**Self-found Studio toast overlap fix (2026-11-20):** Screenshot review and geometry assertions found the success toast and then the error toast could overlap the PDF sheet; a later current-source P0 run also caught a first-frame/resize position stale by one to over 100px. Immediate safe positioning plus ResizeObserver and delayed resize remeasurement now cover that case. `showToast()` now measures the active `.studio-page-sheet`; where a clear gap exists it places feedback below the page, and on desktop it uses a sufficiently wide side margin. When no non-overlapping placement exists, it visually hides the toast while preserving polite/ assertive live-region announcements. Local Chromium success/error checks passed twice at 390×844 and 320×740; P0 rounds 103/104 verified both desktop and mobile restored-document placement below the toolbar with no overlap. Current-image v146 Docker repeated all eight mobile success/error size/orientation checks alongside content-checked 69-page exports. Evidence: `browser-self-found-studio-toast-v2.json`, `browser-self-found-studio-toast-docker-v146.json`, `browser-p0-round99.json`, `browser-p0-round100.json`, `docker-id122-v146-runtime.json`.

Both variants preserved all 69 pages/text/order, page 1/40 thumbnails, selection, navigation, export, 390px width, and zero browser errors. The 1.5× runs recorded first-page readiness of 10.08s/14.43s, maximum long tasks of 581ms/901ms, and 2,110 nonwhite pixels in the rendered page screenshot. The 1.25× runs recorded 10.68s/12.29s, 694ms/857ms maximum long tasks, and 1,678 nonwhite pixels. **Self-found Studio layout-read reduction (2026-11-20, app.js v130 / organizer v18 / shell v64):** Chromium CPU profiles showed repeated `getBoundingClientRect()` calls from `syncStudioMobilePagesPanelPosition` during mode changes. The Organize/Annotate handlers now perform one toolbar-position sync after applying all mode changes, while the page organizer can skip its redundant intermediate sync. Two current-source mobile Chromium rounds verified exactly one geometry measurement per mode switch, the toolbar-aligned Pages panel top, zero 390px overflow, and zero page errors (`browser-id122-toolbar-geometry-batching.json`). The full 69-page profile still shows substantial PDF.js long tasks (max 592/640ms in the current app.js v131 profile), so this optimization does not close ID 122's 250ms target. Post-fix checks passed: P0 60/60 twice, responsive routes 704/704, production image v138 passed two content-checked 69-page exports, and the full suite passed 628 tests twice. ID 122 remains PARTIAL because the 250ms responsiveness target is unmet.



**Self-found mobile Studio scroll-lock fix (2026-11-20, app.js v131 / shell v65):** A 69-page Chromium sequence that switched Annotate → Organize → Annotate and then scrolled to page 40 left `currentPage` stuck at 1 while the canvas was at page 40. The mode handlers queued an asynchronous `jumpToStudioPage` and held the scroll-observer lock while PDF.js rendered the preserved page. Removed that jump: ribbon changes no longer move the canvas or block the next user scroll. Two local 390px mobile Chromium rounds now confirm one geometry measurement per mode switch, Page 40 becomes active, returning to Page 1 works, and a downloaded PDF preserves exact text and order across all 69 pages (`browser-id122-mobile-mode-navigation.json`). Rebuilt production image v138 repeated the mode transitions under 4× CPU throttle and passed Page 40 navigation plus two content/order-checked 69-page exports, four UID-1001 workers, health, zero overflow, and zero errors (`browser-id122-docker-codex-final-v131-v138.json`, `docker-id122-v138-runtime.json`). PWA shell v65 passed two offline rounds with app.js v131. A v131 profile still records 592/640ms maximum long tasks (250ms target unmet), so ID 122 stays PARTIAL. Two current-source P0 rounds passed 60/60, responsive matrix passed 704/704, and the full suite passed 628 twice in the latest v140 run (`browser-p0-app131-round1-20261120.json`, `browser-p0-app131-round2-20261120.json`, `browser-id27-narrow-zoom-layout.json`, `pytest-current-v140-summary.json`).

**ID 122 thumbnail encoding and stale-render fix (2026-11-20):** Current-source profiling identified synchronous thumbnail `canvas.toDataURL()` calls among the Studio hotspots. Two 69-page mobile Chromium rounds preserved page/text order, selection, page 40 navigation, and content-checked exports; both confirmed six visible thumbnail Blob URLs were revoked on clear and a deliberately delayed stale-document render did not repopulate cache. Zero page errors and no 390px overflow. Timing was mixed (readiness 9.28s/7.07s; maximum long tasks 676ms/677ms versus v129 baseline 8.49s/7.81s and 887ms/433ms), so this does not close the 250ms performance target. ID 122 remains PARTIAL. The later local implementation below expands the same workflow to browser-local mode. Evidence: `browser-id37-current-grayscale.json`; runner `browser_id37_current_grayscale.py`.

**ID 37 browser-local Grayscale implementation (2026-11-20):** Added a browser-only path with exact visual color detection, unchanged byte-for-byte output for already-gray files (preserving text), and 150-DPI grayscale raster output for colored files. It caps input at 100 MiB/100 pages, caps cumulative analysis/rendering at 32 million pixels and PNG transfer at 64 MiB, reports raster/text-loss tradeoffs, and yields to the main thread every 128 rows; oversized/password/encrypted inputs can use the existing explicit server fallback. Two Chromium rounds at 1366px and 390px each verified colored-output grayscale (345,600 pixels/output, zero RGB channel difference), exact unchanged gray-file bytes with searchable markers, and zero server jobs/errors. The mobile menu now toggles closed; before the fix its backdrop intercepted result downloads. At that app.js v113 checkpoint, the browser-local audit counted 26/41 workflow variants; the latest route-level source inventory is recorded in ID 37 below. Evidence: `browser-id37-local-grayscale.json`; runner `browser_id37_local_grayscale.py`.

Added a deterministic total-budget regression; the controlled local Chromium run injected an upstream 429 with `Retry-After: 30`, waited 30 seconds, retried, and downloaded a content-checked 10-page Spanish PDF with 10/10 translated markers, no source markers, HTTP 200 and zero browser errors in two rounds. **Previous live provider recheck (2026-11-19; superseded by the latest live recheck above):** A prior run was reported as four successful synthetic Spanish/Hindi downloads, but its JSON artifact was overwritten by the newer mixed matrix; that unretained result is historical only and is not counted as current verification. In a fresh 69-page discriminating-summary probe, live returned HTTP 200, correctly selected the explicit `offline_extractive` fallback, preserved the cancellation instruction and 900000 limit on page 24 and the deadline on page 69, cited pages 1–69, and had zero browser errors (`browser-live-id2-69page-provider.json`). This proves the safe live fallback currently preserves these facts through the API; it does not prove provider-backed synthesis on the deployed app. A live UI probe for the 69-page summary PDF download loaded `/assets/app.js?v=91`, submitted a summarize POST, and remained in “Generating brief…” with no response visible after the 180-second wait. The current local candidate is app.js v113; the deployed browser is therefore stale and does not yet expose the tested local fix. A separate live 5-page AI smoke showed executive summary HTTP 200 but `offline_extractive` mode; Spanish translations were mixed (one content-checked HTTP 200 and one coverage rejection), and both Hindi requests hit HTTP 429. Thus the live provider still has observed variability. IDs 1 and 2 remain PARTIAL pending stable provider-backed live behavior and deployment/retest of the current v113 UI.

**Self-found PWA offline styling defect (ID 357):** the page requested `/assets/styles.css?v=54`, while the offline service-worker shell still precached `v=51`; on activation it purged old shell caches, so an offline reload could not find the new CSS URL. **Latest PDF-to-JPG browser processing:** added zero-upload export of every page at the selected 72/150/300 DPI into a JPG ZIP. The resolution picker now supplies numeric values; browser rendering enforces page, canvas and ZIP-size limits with an explicit server-processing fallback choice. Two local and two current-image Docker Chromium rounds each checked all 44 routes and content-checked the two JPGs in the actual downloaded ZIP at 150 and 300 DPI. Image dimensions were 625×417 and 1250×834 pixels; mean red-channel difference against independent PyMuPDF source renders was 0.501–0.820/255. All browser-local workflows made zero job POSTs, and all rounds had no page errors (`browser-id37-local-text-roundjpg-local-round1.json`, `browser-id37-local-text-roundjpg-local-round2.json`, `browser-id37-local-text-roundjpg-docker-round1.json`, `browser-id37-local-text-roundjpg-docker-round2.json`). Two mobile scan/PWA rounds passed app v103/shell v30. Two full suites passed 589 tests with four existing warnings each (`pytest-local-jpg-final-run1.log`, `pytest-local-jpg-final-run2.log`). Rebuilt production-mode image `sha256:91787fa5992adc7b6cfb8a43dbcc582eb1df381ba1b1233e17ce40ee8b2b9734` was healthy with PID 1 UID 1001. ID 37 now has 19/41 local workflows; 22 remain server-only.

**Latest transparent-scan fix:** server Scan-to-PDF converted transparent PNG pixels to black. Reproduced with a transparent image and fixed server conversion to composite alpha over white paper before enhancement. Two Chromium rounds checked RGBA and palette PNG in browser-local and server modes: all eight downloaded PDFs had white corners, preserved red image content and no browser errors (`browser-scan-transparency.json`). Focused transparency/EXIF tests passed; two full suites passed 589 tests, four existing warnings each (`pytest-scan-alpha-run1.log`, `pytest-scan-alpha-run2.log`). Rebuilt production-mode image `sha256:76f82bba0beb539e3191b711acfcf38db675d0f124c5abf5a9fd1113d26ead61` reached healthy status with PID 1 UID 1001; two more Chromium rounds content-checked eight local/server downloads against that image (`browser-scan-transparency-docker.json`).

**Latest server-processing race fix:** reproduced a server job returning its old result after a replacement file was selected on the same tool. File selection/removal now invalidates the previous workflow, aborts the browser's waiting request, dismisses old progress, and warns that a previously uploaded server job may continue. Old upload callbacks and delayed progress dismissal cannot overwrite a newer workflow. Two controlled Chromium rounds suppressed the stale server result, then submitted the replacement through the real local server and verified its downloaded PDF contains the replacement marker (`browser-server-stale-result.json`); local worker/photo race, 44-route local/fallback matrix and mobile PWA regressions passed. The controlled server test does not prove cancellation of an already uploaded server job. Two complete suites passed 588 tests with four existing warnings (`pytest-server-stale-run1.log`, `pytest-server-stale-run2.log`). Rebuilt image `sha256:e2da884bbd62a49681258d4393491e184d65779b281050de43c1e2a7361de6f0` reached healthy status with PID 1 UID 1001; two browser rounds repeated stale-result suppression and content-checked real replacement downloads (`browser-server-stale-result-docker.json`).

**Latest local-processing file-change fix:** a delayed browser-local operation left its progress indicator visible when a replacement file was selected. Reproduced in Chromium, then added a per-request token: selecting or removing a file invalidates only the prior local request, hides its progress and clears the old result; the new file can run immediately. Two rounds verified the stale result stays hidden and the next download contains only the replacement PDF marker, plus tool-switch and photo-file cases (`browser-local-stale-result.json`). The full 44-route browser matrix, local outputs and consented fallback passed (`browser-id37-local-text-roundfilechange.json`); two mobile PWA rounds passed. Full suite passed twice: 588 tests, four existing warnings each (`pytest-filechange-progress-run1.log`, `pytest-filechange-progress-run2.log`). Rebuilt app v101/shell v28 image `sha256:4181a8c5f4c5595d23b589b88ed8bdef57895114230294831c052bb2e0970c47` reached healthy status with PID 1 UID 1001. The container passed two stale-result/replacement-download rounds and the 44-route content/fallback matrix (`browser-local-stale-result-docker.json`, `browser-id37-local-text-rounddocker-filechange.json`). Its first matrix attempt checked a run button before UI initialization completed; after waiting for the button to become enabled the retry passed.

**Latest local-processing race fix:** changing the selected file or tool while a browser-local operation was running could publish the old result under the new context. Local processing now snapshots its files and checks request identity before displaying output or updating progress; abandoned blob URLs are revoked. Two Chromium rounds delayed a PDF worker and changed its file or tool, and delayed a photo decode while changing its file; all six cases suppressed the stale result, and new-file downloads contained the new marker (`browser-local-stale-result.json`). The 44-route browser matrix content-checked 18 supported local workflows, fallback consent and zero local job posts (`browser-id37-local-text-roundstaleguard.json`); two mobile PWA rounds passed. Full suite passed twice at 588 tests, four existing warnings each (`pytest-stale-result-run1.log`, `pytest-stale-result-run2.log`). Rebuilt image `sha256:c0d024b6334daf3d2d47ce9faa226d1b26ed4000454ef2d07fc69c15af7e5b49` passed the same race and new-file-download checks (`browser-local-stale-result-docker.json`). The first container attempt timed out during browser navigation and Docker health probes timed out three times; health later recovered and the retry passed. One child worker exited during startup and was replaced. Available host memory was approximately 837 MiB when inspected; cause was not established. Verify deployment health under representative load before release.

**Latest camera-image fix:** server Scan-to-PDF previously ignored JPEG EXIF orientation; a camera fixture with orientation 6 produced a sideways 86.4×43.2-point page while local mode produced the intended portrait. The server now applies EXIF orientation before enhancement and page sizing. Two Chromium rounds exercised both local and server modes, parsed the four downloads, checked 43.2×86.4-point pages and sampled the red top/blue bottom image content; no browser errors (`browser-scan-exif-orientation.json`). Focused image tests passed; two full suites passed 588 tests with four existing warnings each (`pytest-scan-exif-run1.log`, `pytest-scan-exif-run2.log`). Rebuilt image `sha256:13bae537a26994a1729969c0cff1848ac23e3fd72ca0abcd0ff15752dbd8c4a5` became healthy in an isolated production-mode container with PID 1 UID 1001. Two more browser rounds repeated both modes against that image, content-checking four correct downloads (`browser-scan-exif-orientation-docker.json`).

**Latest Studio performance experiment:** moving the page observer after the initial render produced 7.32–17.17s first-page readiness in two runs and did not establish a repeatable improvement, so it was reverted (`browser-id122-observer-after-first-trial.json`). Two fresh runs on restored app v99 preserved all 69 pages' text and order, selection and navigation, with first-page readiness of 12.31s / 9.95s and longest tasks of 1365ms / 1069ms (`browser-id122-mobile-69page-studio-export.json`). ## Live deployment drift found during this run

- Latest read-only live drift check found `https://paperplane.qd.je/` serves app.js v91, styles.css v51, and page_organizer.js v14, while this local candidate is app.js v121, styles.css v56, and page_organizer.js v15. Local fixes are therefore not present on the deployed site; repeat the P0/live checks, including Merge, only after the owner deploys. This audit made no deployment or live data/billing mutation. Evidence: `browser-live-v91-readonly-route-check.json` and `browser-live-deploy-drift.json`.

- The locally built Docker image runs healthily with PyMuPDF 1.28.2, Ghostscript 10.05.1, Tesseract 5.5.0, and LibreOffice 25.2.3. A real Chromium session against the isolated local container downloaded and content-checked Merge, Split, PDF/A-1b, and OCR outputs; zero browser errors. The scan OCR fixture began with zero text and returned the three expected phrases. The container used only tmpfs data and dummy local credentials. Evidence: `browser-docker-runtime-smoke.json`; `docker-runtime-verification.json`.
- ID 2 live 69-page retest: two synthetic uploads to the deployed `/ai-intelligence` returned HTTP 200 and citations spanning Pages 1–69, but both responses rendered `Executive Brief (section notes)` with repeated `Executive Summary (Offline Analysis)` blocks. The second live response had no `summary_mode` field and omitted all three page-24/page-69 discriminating facts; the first omitted the 900,000 no-spend limit. The exact same synthetic 69-page fixture on the current local code preserves cancellation, the 900000 amount, and the Page 69 deadline with citations 1–69. Local code path passes; deployed provider-backed behavior remains unverified and must be retested after deployment. Evidence: `browser-live-id2-69page-provider.json`; `browser_id2_local_exact_fixture_fallback.py`; `browser-id2-local-exact-fixture-fallback.json`.

Three focused AI regressions passed; no browser errors. - ID 1 local fallback path: a controlled primary HTTP 429 caused the real local Translate function to invoke the configured secondary provider; the downloaded local result had one page and extracted Spanish text. The local Chromium Admin AI Settings panel visibly displayed that state, with four assertions passing and zero page errors. - Self-review found Docker runs four workers, so the first status implementation's process-local memory was insufficient. Provider health now persists a minimal allowlisted snapshot in shared SQLite settings for all workers, strips secrets and raw provider bodies, and only applies the warning to the currently selected provider. New SQLite readback test passed; Chromium then verified the rate-limit/fallback UI and confirmed the HTML loaded cache-busted `admin.js?v=15` (5/5 checks, zero page errors). Final full suite after this update passed twice: 508 passed each; logs `pytest-cont-id1-run7.log` and `pytest-cont-id1-run8.log`.

- ID 85 mobile result cards: reproduced and checked actual Split and Rotate results at 320×844 and 390×844. Every visible action fit the viewport and remained uncovered; ZIP contents contained two valid one-page PDFs with expected source markers, and the rotated two-page PDF retained its text and 90° page rotations. ## Exact remaining deployment blockers

- **ID 101, owner-only:** rotate the compromised production admin code/key; review the isolated sanitized history-preparation mirror at `C:/Users/mdham/Downloads/pdf_splitter/paperplane-redacted-v23-prep-2026-11-19.git`, then coordinate history rewrite/force-push/cache purge after the v23 code is committed; inspect production Security Audits for unknown successes. Gitleaks CI and the purge script are in code. The new candidate has no remote, excludes all uncommitted v23 changes, and is NOT publish-ready. Do not modify or publish either protected existing history mirror.
- **ID 98, owner-only:** configure Cashfree production keys and perform the single real payment/entitlement check. No real payment was made.
- **ID 1:** controlled local 429→fallback and 98%-character/25%-block rejection pass in Chrome. The latest live matrix returned one content-checked Spanish HTTP 200, one Spanish HTTP 504, and two Hindi HTTP 422 responses at 20% coverage; a separate persisted Spanish download had passed all ten pages in an earlier run. Keep partial until the deployed provider/configuration reliably returns target-language content across the document and the same discrimination matrix passes.
- **ID 2:** live summary returned HTTP 200 with page citations, but the deployed response omitted `summary_mode`; provider-backed synthesis remains unproven, especially for the required 69-page input. Do not describe offline extractive notes as AI synthesis.

4. After deployment, retest the production provider, Drive import, deployed storage survival, security headers, admin feedback delivery, and payment status as listed per ID.

## Self-analysis loop

The latest loop is recorded in `SELF-ANALYSIS.md`. It used fresh profiling of the actual 69-page PDF to identify the dominant Studio cost (PDF.js text drawing/font work) and rejected visual-quality-reducing DPR changes; a ResizeObserver cache removes repeated canvas-width reads, but timing evidence does not establish an improvement and ID 122 stays PARTIAL. A separate P0 geometry run caught a first-frame toast overlap, which was fixed by synchronously positioning the notice and remeasuring after toolbar/page/dock geometry changes. Current app.js v142 P0 browser rounds 103/104 each passed 60/60, with no page errors. The full route matrix passed 704/704, safe-area layout was previously verified in two rounds, and current shell v82 loaded offline twice. The full current suite passed 635 tests in each of two sequential isolated runs. The catalog’s earlier intermittent 359-second PDF-to-Markdown failure remains a documented reliability observation. The 353-item register is not complete: 324 fixed locally, 28 partial, 1 blocked; live/provider, owner-only, and physical-device conditions are still outstanding.
## Full-suite verification

Latest current-source `python -m pytest -q` runs: **635 passed, 4 existing warnings** in each sequential run (`pytest-current-v156-run1.log`, `pytest-current-v156-run2.log`; summary `pytest-current-v156-summary.json`). A preceding v154 attempt had one test assertion mis-scoped to the wrong source block; the assertion was corrected, and v155 then passed 634 tests twice. The current v156 runs include both new Studio geometry-cache and toast-resize regression tests.
## Changed files

V23 product changes include `requirements.txt` (pins PyMuPDF below the incompatible major-version boundary), `ai_engine.py`, `converters.py`, `server.py`, `web/index.html`, `web/assets/app.js`, `web/assets/styles.css`, and `web/assets/swagger-init.js`. Compare now calls its output a `.txt diff file`; PDF/A availability is derived from Ghostscript/ICC prerequisites and accurately disables Run when missing. The mobile Studio canvas was fit above the fixed workspace navigation. An independently spotted mobile Studio restore toast overlap was also fixed by positioning Studio feedback below the toolbar; round 29 verifies no toolbar-button overlap at desktop or 390 px mobile. The browser regression also exposed a stale AI empty state after Clear Chat; `updateAiDocBar()` now refreshes the empty-state visibility, verified in round 31. The Sign PDF tool now supports a drawn image stamp and a printed-name fallback, with a disclosure distinguishing visual marks from cryptographic signatures. Regression tests include `tests/test_launch_audit_v23.py`, `tests/test_admin_security_v9.py`, `tests/test_launch_audit_v10.py`, `tests/test_ai_workflows.py`, `tests/test_launch_audit_v3_hardening.py`, `tests/test_launch_audit_v9.py`, and `tests/test_production_security.py`. Browser harnesses include `reports/launch-audit-v23/browser_p0.py`, `browser_routes.py`, and the new per-item v23 harnesses. The full v22 baseline commit file list is recorded in `V22-BASELINE-FILES.txt`; v22 changes are already part of baseline commit `03436d90d9fc233eedf3a614eaa5de4f520137ca`. V23 evidence artifacts are in this report folder.

This rejects near-identical output but does not prove the live provider can produce clean Spanish/Hindi translations; ID 1 remains PARTIAL. Evidence: `browser-id1-zero-change-rejected.json`, `browser_id1_zero_change_rejected.py`.

- ID 349 follow-up: self-analysis caught an initial helper-placement mistake that caused 18 AI failures in `pytest-final-run6.log`. Moved helpers to module scope; all 74 focused AI workflow and v23 audit tests then passed, and the duplicate-summary browser scenario passed again. The final full suite then passed twice at 541 tests; logs `pytest-final-run10.log` and `pytest-final-run11.log`.

- ID 1 self-review: first final run showed the new no-op gate masked provider-empty and HTTP 429 errors when zero blocks translated. Restricting the content-change gate to outputs with at least one placed translated block restored the existing upstream failure messages; all four focused translation tests then passed. Full-suite runs 10 and 11 each passed 541 tests.


## Follow-up: rotated Crop and Page Numbers gap

Browser-local Crop previously treated display-side margins as unrotated PDF coordinates, causing wrong edges to be cropped on rotated pages. The worker now maps all four margins for 0°, 90°, 180°, and 270° rotation. Two Chromium rounds compared every output page pixel with the requested crop of its original rendered page; all matched, with zero `/api/jobs` posts and no browser errors. Evidence: `browser-id37-crop-rotations.json`, `browser_id37_crop_rotations.py`. Worker/runtime/shell cache versions are now 19/17/15. Three focused cache/processing regressions passed; the two 583-test runs above predate this follow-up.

Resolved in a later follow-up: browser-local Page Numbers now honors the selected background contrast and positions text relative to rotated crop boxes. The four-rotation visual verification is recorded below; this older note is retained as the discovered gap and is no longer an open defect.

Page Numbers follow-up: the earlier OPEN finding above has been addressed for background contrast and rotated crop-box positioning. The local worker now uses a single searchable text operation with fill and outline, selects white outline for dark backgrounds, and positions/rotates text relative to the visible crop box. Two Chromium rounds downloaded a four-page dark fixture with non-default crop boxes and 0°, 90°, 180°, and 270° rotations. Each page's number extracted exactly once at the expected header-left position, white outline pixels were visible, and there were zero API job posts and browser errors. The existing two-round start-page/offset check also passed. Other position/background combinations and physical devices remain outside this particular test. Evidence: `browser-id37-number-rotation-contrast.json`, `browser_id37_number_rotation_contrast.py`, `browser-id37-page-number-options.json`. Current cache versions: app v89, worker v20, PDF runtime v18, shell v16. Full suite passed twice after these changes: 583 passed in 242.45s and 168.28s with four existing warnings per run (`pytest-page-number-contrast-run1.log`, `pytest-page-number-contrast-run2.log`). Prior Docker image and prior PWA tests predate these latest changes.

Latest current-image and PWA follow-up: tested all six Page Numbers positions across four rotated, cropped, dark pages (24 position/page combinations) in local Chromium and again against the rebuilt four-worker production-mode Docker image. Each output's extracted number appeared once at the expected visible-page position, the contrasting outline rendered, and there were no browser errors or `/api/jobs` posts. This covers the dark-background selection and `{n}` format; other background choices and formats are not established by this matrix. Current mobile PWA shell v16/app v89 passed two 390×844 Chromium rounds with content-checked scan PDFs and HTTP 200 offline shell. Docker image `sha256:095a665f08526fa07d92e218566155866e6d7a8ff3f492a243698865c0139e4c` also passed health and local Forms, Metadata, and Crop output checks. The synthetic test container used tmpfs and was stopped. Evidence: `browser-id37-number-position-matrix.json`, `browser-id37-number-position-matrix-docker.json`, `browser-id37-docker-worker-smoke.json`, `browser-id357-mobile-scan-pwa.json`. The latest full-suite runs remain 583 passed twice as recorded above. Item 37 and the overall deployment verdict remain unchanged.

Privacy fallback follow-up: browser processing previously submitted files to the server automatically on a local error despite the on-device disclosure. The app now presents the error and asks for explicit upload confirmation, including the server quota consequence; Cancel leaves the files on the device. It also checks that the operation and file objects still match before scheduling a retry. One complete Chromium round covered 44 routes, 15 content-checked local outputs, cancellation with zero upload, and an approved fallback with one server job and a validated downloaded PDF. Focused three regressions passed. Two further attempts to repeat the entire browser run failed during local navigation/startup and then a missing temporary fixture; the second full round and latest full-suite/PWA/Docker verification remain outstanding. Evidence: `browser-id37-local-text-round1.json`, `browser_id37_id242_local_processing.py`. Current app/shell cache versions are v90/v17.

Privacy fallback verification follow-up: after the earlier slow-machine timeouts, the full 44-route Chromium harness completed a fresh second round on current source. All 15 local downloads were content-checked with zero job posts; declining server fallback made zero posts, and explicitly approving it made one job post and returned a content-checked PDF. The earlier first round checked the same choices before the final request-identity scheduling guard; that guard has not yet been separately exercised by changing files while confirmation is open. The current full suite passed twice: 583 tests in 346.52s and 151.81s, with four existing deprecation warnings each (`pytest-privacy-fallback-run1.log`, `pytest-privacy-fallback-run2.log`). Current PWA shell v17/app v90 passed two mobile scan/offline-shell rounds. Rebuilt four-worker production-mode Docker image `sha256:bf351677501c81d75ba163d954f96eb12e911a5952b963931423c1ce03a390a1`; health was `ok`, and Chromium content-checked Forms, Metadata and Crop with no job posts or browser errors. Container used synthetic settings and tmpfs, then stopped. Evidence: `browser-id37-local-text-round2.json`, `browser-id357-mobile-scan-pwa.json`, `browser-id37-docker-worker-smoke.json`.

Privacy fallback race verification: a fresh current-source Chromium run exercised all 44 routes and 15 content-checked local workflows, cancellation, approved server fallback, and an asynchronous file-order change while the upload confirmation was open. After the selection changed, accepting the old confirmation produced no additional `/api/jobs` request, and browser processing remained selected. No browser page errors occurred. Evidence: `browser-id37-local-text-roundrace.json`, `browser_id37_id242_local_processing.py`. Privacy fallback operation-switch regression: self-analysis found a second state leak after an approved fallback had started: changing tools could leave the next operation in server-upload mode. Operation changes now clear the fallback state and restore the selected local-processing preference; early validation exits also clear stale fallback state. A two-round current-source Chrome test held the approved submission in flight, switched from Merge to Rotate, and confirmed the local toggle was restored, the fallback flag was clear, no `/api/jobs` request was issued, and there were no page errors. Evidence: `browser-id37-fallback-switch.json`, `browser_id37_fallback_switch.py`. Current asset cache versions are app v91 and service-worker shell v18. The full current-source suite passed twice: 583 passed, four existing warnings each (`pytest-final-switch-run1.log`, 267.78s; `pytest-final-switch-run2.log`, 254.43s). This closes the current local regression loop only; the v23 audit remains NOT DEPLOYMENT-READY until the remaining partial/live/owner conditions and verification gates are closed.

ID 37 browser-local text watermark follow-up: added standard-font text watermarking to the PDF worker and exposed the browser-local option on the Watermark PDF route. Image watermark selection remains server-side only and is disclosed before upload consent. Current-source Chromium runs downloaded and parsed a four-page PDF with rotations 0°/90°/180°/270°; each page retained its source marker and selectable watermark, rendered the configured red color, placed the mark near page center, and generated zero `/api/jobs` posts or browser errors. An image-watermark run showed the upload confirmation; cancel produced zero jobs. Cache versions are app v93, runtime v19, worker v21, shell v20. The two latest full-suite runs each passed 583 tests (four existing deprecation warnings), and the current mobile PWA passed two rounds. Evidence: `browser-id37-watermark-round3.json` through `browser-id37-watermark-round6.json`, `pytest-id37-watermark-run2.log`, `pytest-id37-watermark-run3.log`, `browser-id357-mobile-scan-pwa.json`, `browser_id37_local_watermark.py`.

ID 122 scroll-path optimization: the low-end 69-page browser profile showed Studio's active-page scroll handler scanned every page slot and read every bounding rectangle on each scroll. It now binary-searches the ordered page-slot collection and updates only the old/new active slot instead of querying/mutating every page canvas. Two 4× CPU Chromium rounds scrolled to page 40 and back to page 1, rendered both targets, exported all 69 pages with exact text/order, and recorded 8 page-slot geometry reads for the measured scroll (down from 69 page-slot reads per handler pass). Load-to-first-page was 6.15s/4.88s; largest observed tasks were 691ms/430ms, so ID 122 remains PARTIAL pending further PDF.js main-thread work and physical-device profiling. A 60% prefetch-window experiment showed no repeatable gain and was reverted; see `browser-id122-prefetch60-trial.json`. Current app/shell versions are v92/v19. Focused regressions passed (3 tests), and the complete 583-test suite passed twice with four existing warnings (`pytest-id122-scroll-optimization-run1.log`, 162.24s; `pytest-id122-scroll-optimization-run2.log`, 128.42s). Evidence: `browser-id122-mobile-69page-studio-export.json`, `browser_id122_mobile_69page_studio_export.py`, `browser-id357-mobile-scan-pwa.json`, tests in `tests/test_launch_audit_v23.py`.

**Self-found DOM XSS hardening (2026-11-19):** A targeted source review found `key_prefix` from `/api/developer/keys` interpolated unescaped into HTML in both the Settings and Developer API-key lists. Both paths now use `escapeHtml`; added regression coverage and bumped app.js to v118 and PWA shell to v50. Chromium injected a synthetic `<img onerror>` prefix into both list renderers: the payload remained visible text, created zero image nodes, did not execute, and produced zero page errors. Two offline Chromium rounds then verified shell v50 loaded app.js v118 and styles.css v56 with HTTP 200 and zero page errors. The entire suite passed twice at 610 tests, four existing deprecation warnings per run (`pytest-id-security-xss-run1.log`, `pytest-id-security-xss-run2.log`). This is an extra codebase hardening fix; it does not change the v23 register counts or resolve its remaining live/owner gates. Evidence: `browser-security-api-key-prefix-xss.json`, `browser-id357-offline-v50-smoke.json`, `tests/test_launch_audit_v23.py::test_developer_api_key_prefix_is_escaped_before_html_rendering`.


**Follow-up AI extraction DOM hardening (2026-11-19):** The source review found two more provider-controlled values entering the invoice extraction HTML as raw text: line-item `quantity` and `source_pages`. Both are now encoded with `escapeHtml`; added a regression test. Real Chromium invoked the production extraction renderer with a synthetic `<img onerror>` payload in both fields: payloads remained text, zero image nodes were created, no handler ran, and the page had no errors (`browser-security-extraction-values-xss.json`). App.js is now v119 and the offline shell is v51; two offline Chromium rounds loaded the exact cached app/styles successfully (`browser-id357-offline-v51-smoke.json`). P0 browser matrix passed 60/60 with zero page errors (`browser-p0.json`). The complete suite passed twice at 611 tests, four existing warnings per run (`pytest-id-extraction-xss-run1.log`, `pytest-id-extraction-xss-run2.log`). This is extra self-found hardening and does not close the other open v23 list items or owner gates.


**Self-found authentication and extraction DOM hardening (2026-11-19, latest candidate):** Replaced password-recovery response HTML interpolation with DOM nodes and `textContent`; a development reset link is rendered only for a same-origin root URL with a `reset_token` query. Chromium verified a hostile message stays text, a `javascript:` reset URL is suppressed, and a valid same-origin synthetic reset link remains clickable; zero page errors (`browser-security-password-reset-response-xss.json`). Together with the previous API-key prefix and AI-extraction quantity/source-page escaping fixes, this hardens three independent response-rendering paths. Candidate cache versions are app.js v120 / shell v52. Two PWA offline rounds loaded v120/v52 and CSS v56 with HTTP 200; current-source P0 matrix passed 60/60, zero errors (`browser-id357-offline-v52-smoke.json`, `browser-p0.json`). Full test suite passed twice at 612 tests, with four existing deprecation warnings each (`pytest-id-reset-xss-run1.log`, `pytest-id-reset-xss-run2.log`). These extra fixes do not change the v23 list counts; the remaining PARTIAL/BLOCKED and owner/live gates remain open.


**Password recovery response safety follow-up (2026-11-19):** Removed the last identified reflected HTML sink in the forgot-password client. Server-provided status text now uses `textContent`; a development reset link is accepted only when the parsed URL is same-origin, uses the root path, and includes `reset_token`. Chromium tested hostile markup, a `javascript:` URL, and a valid same-origin token URL; hostile content remained inert and the valid link remained available. Latest app.js v120 / PWA shell v52 passed two offline Chromium reloads (HTTP 200, no errors), the current P0 matrix (60/60, no errors), and the full suite twice (612 passed each; four existing deprecation warnings). Evidence: `browser-security-password-reset-response-xss.json`, `browser-id357-offline-v52-smoke.json`, `browser-p0.json`, `pytest-id-reset-xss-run1.log`, `pytest-id-reset-xss-run2.log`.


**Self-found command-palette DOM injection fix (2026-11-20):** Source audit found operation keys, labels, descriptions, and categories interpolated directly into command-palette `innerHTML`. All four values now pass through the HTML escaper, and the app cache key advanced to v122 with offline shell v55. Current-source Chromium injected a hostile `<img onerror>` value into every field and the operation key: all values remained inert text, no image or handler was created, normal palette rendering and keyboard semantics remained, and there were zero page errors or 390px overflow. The matching focused regression passed; the full suite passed twice at 615 tests (four existing warnings each). Fresh P0 Chromium passed 60/60 with no page errors; offline mobile shell reload passed 2/2 with app.js v122 and CSS v56. Evidence: `browser-command-palette-xss.json`, `browser-id357-service-worker-v55-offline-local.json`, `browser-p0.json`, `pytest-domslots-v122-run1.log`, `pytest-domslots-v122-run2.log`. **Self-found watermark image resource guard (2026-11-20):** The server-side image-watermark path decoded arbitrary base64 data and Pillow decoded it without a pixel bound. Added a 25 MiB encoded/decoded byte limit, strict PNG/JPEG data-URL and base64 validation before temporary-file creation, and a 20-megapixel decode ceiling before RGBA conversion; temporary files are cleaned on errors. Two Chromium rounds on isolated local servers exercised the `/api/jobs` upload path: an adversarial 24-megapixel PNG failed with the specific actionable limit error; a normal PNG watermark completed, downloaded HTTP 200, preserved source text, and contained the expected red watermark pixels. Both rounds had zero browser errors (`browser-id361-watermark-image-guard.json`). The full test suite passed twice at 617 tests, with four existing deprecation warnings per run. This hardens the processing path but does not resolve historical ID 28's reported 20% stall; its cause remains unproven and the item remains PARTIAL. Existing release register remains 322 FIXED LOCALLY, 30 PARTIAL, 1 BLOCKED; deployment remains HOLD.

## Current app.js v142 self-found Studio follow-up (2026-11-20)

A repeated P0 browser run exposed that the restored-document toast could first paint at its generic 76px top, overlapping a variable-height Studio toolbar. The initial delayed-reposition attempt did not meet first-frame behavior and is superseded. Current code synchronously establishes a clear position before the toast is shown, uses rounded toolbar clearance, repositions on window resize and on ResizeObserver changes to the toolbar, page sheet and dock, then disconnects observers when the notice expires. Chromium P0 rounds 103 and 104 each passed 60 checks with no browser errors; restore toasts sat below desktop toolbar (top 242px vs bottom 198px) and mobile toolbar (top 226.6/227.5px vs bottom 182px), with no overlapping buttons. The earlier failed round 102 is retained as reproduction evidence for the bug. The controlled 69-page Studio profile on final app.js v142 preserved page text/order and export in both rounds, but maximum long tasks were 633ms and 472ms (target <250ms still unmet). Evidence: `browser-p0-round103.json`, `browser-p0-round104.json`, `browser-id122-mobile-69page-studio-export_v142_final_20261120.json`, `browser-id122-canvas-area-width-cache.json`.

## Railway dynamic-port production-image fix (2026-11-22)

A self-found deployment test exposed that the app ignored Railway's injected `PORT` because the Docker image set `PAPERPLANE_PORT=8501`. Updated both launch paths to prefer `PORT`, and, only when `RAILWAY_SERVICE_ID` is set, allow Railway's `healthcheck.railway.app` Host. A local server started with `PORT=18527` and `PAPERPLANE_PORT=8501` returned `/api/health` HTTP 200 on port 18527. The first fresh image v153 proved the app and Railway health host returned 200 but uncovered a second mismatch: the Dockerfile HEALTHCHECK remained hardcoded to 8501 and marked the container unhealthy (`docker-v153-railway-health-repro.json`). Updated the Docker HEALTHCHECK to use the injected port.

Fresh production-mode image v154 (`sha256:ad66d2ecb4585a666169c85b1ba7f9d7eb8a62e4509fa4010c3a406596120d3b`) ran with `PORT=18529` and `PAPERPLANE_PORT=8501`; app health and Railway Host each returned 200, and Docker health became `healthy`. Two Chromium rounds tested all 44 operations: 41 content-checked downloads each, one SSRF block each, two provider-only actions unavailable without credentials, and zero page errors. The temporary container and tmpfs were removed. Latest full suite passed 646 tests twice, four existing warnings per run (`pytest-current-v164-run1.log`, `pytest-current-v164-run2.log`). Evidence: `docker-v154-railway-runtime.json`, `browser-id28-v148-current-image-catalog-current-v154-railway-2026-11-22.json`, `docker-build-v154.log`.

## Live browser recheck (2026-11-22, CUA + Playwright)

Used the user's Chrome session first, then performed a separate Playwright verification against `https://paperplane.qd.je`. Chrome showed deployed app.js v154 and styles.css v61; `/api/health` returned HTTP 200 with status `ok`, four workers, 500 MB max upload, `auth_required=false`, and a reported 24-hour job TTL. The 24-hour value should be reconciled with the configured retention policy before release. The homepage, Merge, Tool Desk, Pricing, Studio, FAQ, and health page loaded. Chrome console error/warning capture was empty.

The Playwright live smoke ran two rounds across six routes at 320, 390, and 1024 CSS pixels (36 checks): all returned HTTP 200, no document-level horizontal overflow, zero page errors, and zero console errors. A second two-round probe covered Merge and Studio at 320, 390, 768, 1024, and 1366 pixels (20 checks); no document overflow or topbar overlap was found. Studio's primary and secondary tool ribbons at 390px are horizontally scrollable (372px viewport; 492px/729px content), so controls extend outside the viewport until users swipe those rows. Evidence: `live-cua-browser-2026-11-22.json`, `live-playwright-smoke-2026-11-22.json`, `live-playwright-topbar-2026-11-22.json`.

The requested live synthetic PDF upload could not be completed: Chrome blocked local-file selection because the ChatGPT extension does not have `Allow access to file URLs` enabled. No file was uploaded and no job was submitted. To continue that live content/download test, the user must enable it at `chrome://extensions` → ChatGPT extension → Details → **Allow access to file URLs**, then rerun the live upload. Current local app.js is v155 while live serves v154; local-only v155 fixes still need the user's deployment and a post-deploy retest.
